# CSS Text Level 4: whitespace, character alignment and hyphenation clauses

This is an explicitly partial reference capture of the [CSS Text Level 4 Editor’s Draft](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/), retrieved on 2026-10-10. It preserves the original metadata and legal notice, followed by complete sections 4.1 (`white-space-collapsing`), 4.3 (`white-space-rules`, including both phases and segment-break transformation), 6.3.5 (`hyphenate-line-limits`), 6.4 (`overflow-wrap-property`), 7.1 (`text-align-property`) and 7.2 (`character-alignment`). Other clauses are outside this capture; links to them resolve to the upstream document. This is source evidence, not an assertion that unresolved draft questions have normative answers.

The full retrieved HTML identifies revision `4f200bd6e3bd48ea9fb923b4f98f92a9bbfbb04c`, and its displayed draft date is 14 August 2026. Full HTML: 3,350,194 bytes, SHA-256 `84295064951605c856935d2216b8952060726da6794412c8c4ab35a86a298f64`. The complete selected-clause HTML container (with an explicit UTF-8 charset declaration) is 850,801 bytes, SHA-256 `2b0d3e0d5174dbf8fa50f469861183cb7d257563829d8b3184a545eb35b335e4`.

Conversion uses the repository’s [HTML-to-Markdown tools](tools/html-to-markdown/README.md) and installed Pandoc 3.1.11.1 with its semantic Lua filter. The five ordinary property tables remain tables. Source headings, IDs, literal blocks, text, variables, links and table cells are checked against the extracted HTML after Markdown rendering. The original W3C notice below remains part of the capture; see also the local [W3C Software and Document License](../licenses/w3c/software-license-2023.txt).

### <a id="white-space-collapsing"></a>4.1.  White Space Collapsing: the <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-collapse④"></a>[white-space-collapse](#propdef-white-space-collapse) property[](#white-space-collapsing)

Tests

This section has limited direct coverage, but extensive coverage through the 'white-space' shorthand. Missing tests: \* any direct tests as a longhand (other than syntax and inheritance) \* tests of the preserve-spaces value (direct or indirect) \* tests of the discard value (direct or indirect)

- [white-space-collapse-invalid.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/parsing/white-space-collapse-invalid.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/parsing/white-space-collapse-invalid.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/parsing/white-space-collapse-invalid.html)

This property specifies whether and how [white space](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#white-space-processing) is collapsed. Values have the following meanings, which must be interpreted according to the [White Space Processing Rules](#white-space-rules):

<strong><a id="valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse"></a><strong>collapse</strong></strong>

This value directs user agents to collapse sequences of <a id="ref-for-white-space⑥"></a>[white space](#white-space) into a single character (or [in some cases](#line-break-transform), no character).

------------------------------------------------------------------------

<strong><a id="valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve"></a><strong>preserve</strong></strong>

This value prevents user agents from collapsing sequences of <a id="ref-for-white-space⑦"></a>[white space](#white-space). <a id="ref-for-segment-break④"></a>[Segment breaks](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) such as line feeds are preserved as <a id="ref-for-forced-line-break①"></a>[forced line breaks](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#forced-line-break).

<strong><a id="valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-breaks"></a><strong>preserve-breaks</strong></strong>

Like <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse①"></a>[collapse](#valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse), this value collapses consecutive <a id="ref-for-white-space⑧"></a>[white space characters](#white-space), but preserves <a id="ref-for-segment-break⑤"></a>[segment breaks](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) in the source as <a id="ref-for-forced-line-break②"></a>[forced line breaks](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#forced-line-break).

Tests

Direct tests as a longhand:

- [white-space-collapse-preserve-breaks-001.xht](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-collapse-preserve-breaks-001.xht) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-collapse-preserve-breaks-001.xht) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-collapse-preserve-breaks-001.xht)

------------------------------------------------------------------------

<strong><a id="valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-spaces"></a><strong>preserve-spaces</strong></strong>

This value prevents user agents from collapsing sequences of <a id="ref-for-white-space⑨"></a>[white space](#white-space), and converts <a id="ref-for-tabs①"></a>[tabs](#tabs) and <a id="ref-for-segment-break⑥"></a>[segment breaks](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) to <a id="ref-for-spaces②"></a>[spaces](#spaces). (This value is intended to represent the behavior of <code>xml&#58;space=&#34;preserve&#34;</code> in SVG.)

<strong><a id="valdef-white-space-collapse-break-spaces"></a><strong>break-spaces</strong></strong>

The behavior is identical to that of <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve②"></a>[preserve](#valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve), except that:

- Any sequence of <a id="ref-for-preserved-white-space②"></a>[preserved](#preserved-white-space) <a id="ref-for-white-space①⓪"></a>[white space](#white-space) or <a id="ref-for-other-space-separators③"></a>[other space separators](#other-space-separators) always takes up space, including at the end of the line.
  Tests

  - [white-space-intrinsic-size-001.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-intrinsic-size-001.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-intrinsic-size-001.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-intrinsic-size-001.html)
  - [white-space-intrinsic-size-002.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-intrinsic-size-002.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-intrinsic-size-002.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-intrinsic-size-002.html)
  - [white-space-intrinsic-size-021.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-intrinsic-size-021.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-intrinsic-size-021.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-intrinsic-size-021.html)
- A <a id="ref-for-soft-wrap-opportunity①"></a>[soft wrap opportunity](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#soft-wrap-opportunity) exists after every <a id="ref-for-preserved-white-space③"></a>[preserved](#preserved-white-space) <a id="ref-for-white-space①①"></a>[white space](#white-space) character and after every <a id="ref-for-other-space-separators④"></a>[other space separator](#other-space-separators) (including between adjacent spaces).

Note: This value does not guarantee that there will never be any overflow due to white space: for example, if the line length is so short that even a single white space character does not fit, overflow is unavoidable.

<strong><a id="valdef-white-space-collapse-discard"></a><strong>discard</strong></strong>

This value directs user agents to “discard” all white space in the element.

Tests

- [white-space-collapse-discard-001.xht](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-collapse-discard-001.xht) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-collapse-discard-001.xht) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/white-space/white-space-collapse-discard-001.xht)

<a id="issue-e3dd9c3a"></a>

<strong>Issue:</strong>

[](#issue-e3dd9c3a) Does this preserve line break opportunities or no? Do we need a distinct "hide" value? If it preserves line break opportunities, maybe it should be replaced with a <a id="ref-for-propdef-word-space-transform③"></a>[word-space-transform](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-word-space-transform) value?

<a id="ref-for-white-space①②"></a>[White space](#white-space) that was not removed or collapsed due to white space processing is called <a id="preserved-white-space"></a><strong>preserved white space</strong>.

<a id="example-6af3efc0"></a>

<strong>Example:</strong>

[](#example-6af3efc0)

The following style rules implement MathML’s white space processing:

``` text
@namespace m "http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML";
m|* {
  white-space-collapse: discard;
}
m|mi, m|mn, m|mo, m|ms, m|mtext {
  white-space-trim: discard-inner;
}
```

### <a id="white-space-rules"></a>4.3.  The White Space Processing Rules[](#white-space-rules)

Tests

This section has good test coverage. Most tests to be found in subsections.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Except where specified otherwise, white space processing in CSS affects only the <a id="white-space"></a><strong>document white space characters</strong>: <a id="spaces"></a><strong>spaces</strong> (U+0020), <a id="tabs"></a><strong>tabs</strong> (U+0009), and [segment breaks](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#white-space-processing).

Note: The set of characters considered <a id="ref-for-white-space①④"></a>[document white space](#white-space) (part of the document content) and those considered syntactic white space (part of the CSS syntax) are not necessarily identical. However, since both include spaces (U+0020), tabs (U+0009), and line feeds (U+000A) most authors won’t notice any differences.

Besides space (U+0020) and no-break space (U+00A0), Unicode defines a number of additional space separator characters. [\[UNICODE\]](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#biblio-unicode) In this specification all characters in the Unicode <a id="ref-for-unicode-general-category③"></a>[general category](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#unicode-general-category) Zs except space (U+0020) and no-break space (U+00A0) are collectively referred to as <a id="other-space-separators"></a><strong>other space separators</strong>.

#### <a id="white-space-phase-1"></a>4.3.1.  Phase I: Collapsing and Transformation[](#white-space-phase-1)

Tests

This section has good test coverage, most parts are well exercised. Missing tests: \* If white-space-collapse is set to preserve-spaces, each tab and segment break is converted to a space.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Note: <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-trim⑦"></a>[white-space-trim](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-white-space-trim) is taken into account prior to this phase.

For each inline (including anonymous inlines; see [CSS 2 § 9.2.2.1 Anonymous inline boxes](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/visuren.html#anonymous) [\[CSS2\]](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#biblio-css2)) within an <a id="ref-for-inline-formatting-context"></a>[inline formatting context](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-display-4/#inline-formatting-context), <a id="ref-for-white-space①⑤"></a>[white space characters](#white-space) are processed as follows prior to <a id="ref-for-line-breaking-process"></a>[line breaking](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#line-breaking-process) and [bidi reordering](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#text-direction), ignoring <a id="bidi-formatting-characters"></a><strong>bidi formatting characters</strong> (characters with the <code>Bidi&#95;Control</code> property [\[UAX9\]](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#biblio-uax9)) as if they were not there:

- <a id="collapse"></a> [](#collapse) If <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-collapse⑤"></a>[white-space-collapse](#propdef-white-space-collapse) is set to <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse②"></a>[collapse](#valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse) or <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-breaks①"></a>[preserve-breaks](#valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-breaks), <a id="ref-for-white-space①⑥"></a>[white space characters](#white-space) are considered <a id="collapsible-white-space"></a><strong>collapsible</strong> and are processed by performing the following steps:
  1.  Any sequence of collapsible <a id="ref-for-spaces③"></a>[spaces](#spaces) and <a id="ref-for-tabs②"></a>[tabs](#tabs) immediately preceding or following a <a id="ref-for-segment-break⑨"></a>[segment break](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) is removed.

- If <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-collapse⑥"></a>[white-space-collapse](#propdef-white-space-collapse) is set to <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-spaces"></a>[preserve-spaces](#valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-spaces), each <a id="ref-for-tabs④"></a>[tab](#tabs) and <a id="ref-for-segment-break①①"></a>[segment break](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) is converted to a <a id="ref-for-spaces⑧"></a>[space](#spaces).
- If <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-collapse⑦"></a>[white-space-collapse](#propdef-white-space-collapse) is set to <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve③"></a>[preserve](#valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve) or <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-spaces①"></a>[preserve-spaces](#valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-spaces), any sequence of spaces is treated as a sequence of non-breaking spaces except that a <a id="ref-for-soft-wrap-opportunity④"></a>[soft wrap opportunity](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#soft-wrap-opportunity) exists at the end of each maximal sequence of <a id="ref-for-spaces⑨"></a>[spaces](#spaces) and/or <a id="ref-for-tabs⑤"></a>[tabs](#tabs). For <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-break-spaces①"></a>[break-spaces](#valdef-white-space-collapse-break-spaces), a <a id="ref-for-soft-wrap-opportunity⑤"></a>soft wrap opportunity exists after every <a id="ref-for-spaces①⓪"></a>space and every <a id="ref-for-tabs⑥"></a>tab.

<a id="egbidiwscollapse"></a>

<strong>Example:</strong>

[](#egbidiwscollapse) The following example illustrates the interaction of white-space collapsing and bidirectionality. Consider the following markup fragment, taking special note of <a id="ref-for-spaces①①"></a>[spaces](#spaces) (with varied backgrounds and borders for emphasis and identification):

``` text
<ltr>A <rtl> B </rtl> C</ltr>
```

where the <code>&#60;ltr&#62;</code> element represents a left-to-right embedding and the <code>&#60;rtl&#62;</code> element represents a right-to-left embedding. If the <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space④"></a>[white-space](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-white-space) property is set to <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-normal①"></a>[normal](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#valdef-white-space-normal), the white-space processing model will result in the following:

- The <a id="ref-for-spaces①②"></a>[space](#spaces) before the B ( ) will collapse with the <a id="ref-for-spaces①③"></a>space after the A ( ).
- The <a id="ref-for-spaces①④"></a>[space](#spaces) before the C ( ) will collapse with the <a id="ref-for-spaces①⑤"></a>space after the B ( ).

This will leave two <a id="ref-for-spaces①⑥"></a>[spaces](#spaces), one after the A in the left-to-right embedding level, and one after the B in the right-to-left embedding level. The text will then be ordered according to the Unicode bidirectional algorithm, with the end result being:

``` text
A  BC
```

Note that there will be two <a id="ref-for-spaces①⑦"></a>[spaces](#spaces) between A and B, and none between B and C. This is best avoided by putting <a id="ref-for-spaces①⑧"></a>spaces outside the element instead of just inside the opening and closing tags and, where practical, by relying on implicit bidirectionality instead of explicit embedding levels.

Tests

- [white-space-bidirectionality-001.xht](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/CSS2/text/white-space-bidirectionality-001.xht) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/CSS2/text/white-space-bidirectionality-001.xht) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/CSS2/text/white-space-bidirectionality-001.xht)

#### <a id="white-space-phase-2"></a>4.3.2.  Phase II: Trimming and Positioning[](#white-space-phase-2)

Tests

This section has good test coverage, all parts are well exercised.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

Then, the entire block is rendered. Inlines are laid out, taking [bidi reordering](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#text-direction) into account, and <a id="ref-for-wrapping①"></a>[wrapping](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#wrapping) as specified by the <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-wrap-mode③"></a>[text-wrap-mode](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-text-wrap-mode) and <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-wrap-style"></a>[text-wrap-style](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-text-wrap-style) property. As each line is laid out,

1.  A sequence of <a id="ref-for-collapsible-white-space④"></a>[collapsible](#collapsible-white-space) <a id="ref-for-spaces①⑨"></a>[spaces](#spaces) at the beginning of a line is removed.

2.  If the <a id="ref-for-tab-size-dfn"></a>[tab size](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#tab-size-dfn) is zero, <a id="ref-for-preserved-white-space④"></a>[preserved](#preserved-white-space) <a id="ref-for-tabs⑦"></a>[tabs](#tabs) are not rendered. Otherwise, each <a id="ref-for-preserved-white-space⑤"></a>preserved <a id="ref-for-tabs⑧"></a>tab is rendered as a horizontal shift that lines up the start edge of the next glyph with the next <a id="ref-for-tab-stop"></a>[tab stop](#tab-stop). If this distance is less than 0.5ch, then the subsequent <a id="ref-for-tab-stop①"></a>tab stop is used instead. <a id="tab-stop"></a><strong>Tab stops</strong> occur at points that are multiples of the <a id="ref-for-tab-size-dfn①"></a>tab size from the starting content edge of the <a id="ref-for-preserved-white-space⑥"></a>preserved <a id="ref-for-tabs⑨"></a>tab’s nearest <a id="ref-for-block-container①"></a>[block container](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-display-4/#block-container) ancestor. The <a id="ref-for-tab-size-dfn②"></a>tab size is given by the <a id="ref-for-propdef-tab-size"></a>[tab-size](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-tab-size) property.

    - If <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-collapse①⓪"></a>[white-space-collapse](#propdef-white-space-collapse) is <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve④"></a>[preserve](#valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve) and <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-wrap-mode④"></a>[text-wrap-mode](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-text-wrap-mode) is not <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-wrap-mode-nowrap①"></a>[nowrap](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#valdef-text-wrap-mode-nowrap), the UA must (unconditionally) <a id="ref-for-hang②"></a>[hang](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#hang) this sequence, unless the sequence is followed by a <a id="ref-for-forced-line-break③"></a>[forced line break](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#forced-line-break), in which case it must <a id="ref-for-conditionally-hang"></a>[conditionally hang](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#conditionally-hang) the sequence instead. It may also visually collapse the character advance widths of any that would otherwise overflow.

    - If <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-collapse①①"></a>[white-space-collapse](#propdef-white-space-collapse) is set to <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-break-spaces②"></a>[break-spaces](#valdef-white-space-collapse-break-spaces), <a id="ref-for-spaces②②"></a>[spaces](#spaces), <a id="ref-for-tabs①①"></a>[tabs](#tabs), and <a id="ref-for-other-space-separators⑥"></a>[other space separators](#other-space-separators) are treated the same as other visible characters: they cannot <a id="ref-for-hang④"></a>[hang](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#hang) nor have their advance width collapsed.

    <a id="issue-a72a5cd8"></a>

    <strong>Issue:</strong>

    [](#issue-a72a5cd8) What should happen here for <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-collapse①②"></a>[white-space-collapse: preserve-spaces](#propdef-white-space-collapse)?

<a id="example-af2745cd"></a>

<strong>Example:</strong>

[](#example-af2745cd) This example shows that <a id="ref-for-conditionally-hang①"></a>[conditionally hanging](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#conditionally-hang) white space at the end of lines with forced breaks provides symmetry with the start of the line. An underline is added to help visualize the spaces.

``` text
p {
  white-space: pre-wrap;
  width: 5ch;
  border: solid 1px;
  font-family: monospace;
  text-align: center;
}
```

``` text
<p> 0 </p>
```

The sample above would be rendered as follows:

0

Since the final <a id="ref-for-spaces②③"></a>[space](#spaces) is before a forced line break and does not overflow, it does not hang, and centering works as expected.

<a id="example-e231f51f"></a>

<strong>Example:</strong>

[](#example-e231f51f) This example illustrates the difference between <a id="ref-for-hang⑤"></a>[hanging](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#hang) <a id="ref-for-spaces②④"></a>[spaces](#spaces) at the end of lines without forced breaks, and <a id="ref-for-conditionally-hang②"></a>[conditionally hanging](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#conditionally-hang) them at the end of lines with forced breaks. An underline is added to help visualize the <a id="ref-for-spaces②⑤"></a>spaces.

``` text
p {
  white-space: pre-wrap;
  width: 3ch;
  border: solid 1px;
  font-family: monospace;
}
```

``` text
<p> 0 0 0 0 </p>
```

The sample above would be rendered as follows:

0  
0 0  
0

If <code>p { text-align&#58; right; }</code> was added, the result would be as follows:

0   
0 0   
0

As the <a id="ref-for-preserved-white-space⑧"></a>[preserved](#preserved-white-space) <a id="ref-for-spaces②⑥"></a>[spaces](#spaces) at the end of lines without a forced break must <a id="ref-for-hang⑥"></a>[hang](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#hang), they are not considered when placing the rest of the line during text alignment. When aligning towards the end, this means any such <a id="ref-for-spaces②⑦"></a>spaces will overflow, and will not prevent the rest of the line’s content from being flush with the edge of the line. On the other hand, preserved spaces at the end of a line <em>with</em> a forced break <a id="ref-for-conditionally-hang③"></a>[conditionally hang](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#conditionally-hang). Since the space at the end of the last line would not overflow in this example, it does not <a id="ref-for-hang⑦"></a>hang and therefore is considered during text alignment.

<a id="example-6c2f4658"></a>

<strong>Example:</strong>

[](#example-6c2f4658) In the following example, there is not enough room on any line to fit the end-of-line spaces, so they <a id="ref-for-hang⑧"></a>[hang](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#hang) on all lines: the one on the line without a forced break because it must, as well as the one on the line with a forced break, because it <a id="ref-for-conditionally-hang④"></a>[conditionally hangs](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#conditionally-hang) and overflows. An underline is added to help visualize the spaces.

``` text
p {
  white-space: pre-wrap;
  width: 3ch;
  border: solid 1px;
  font-family: monospace;
}
```

``` text
<p>0 0 0 0 </p>
```

0 0  
0 0

The last line is not wrapped before the last <code>0</code> because characters that <a id="ref-for-conditionally-hang⑤"></a>[conditionally hang](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#conditionally-hang) are not considered when measuring the line’s contents for fit.

#### <a id="line-break-transform"></a>4.3.3.  Segment Break Transformation Rules[](#line-break-transform)

Tests

This section has reasonable test coverage, though some assertions are only tested indirectly through test for other features that rely on this, rather than by dedicated tests.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

When <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-collapse①③"></a>[white-space-collapse](#propdef-white-space-collapse) is not <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse⑤"></a>[collapse](#valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse), <a id="ref-for-segment-break①②"></a>[segment breaks](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) are not <a id="ref-for-collapsible-white-space⑦"></a>[collapsible](#collapsible-white-space). For values other than <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse⑥"></a>collapse or <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-spaces②"></a>[preserve-spaces](#valdef-white-space-collapse-preserve-spaces) (which transforms them into <a id="ref-for-spaces②⑧"></a>[spaces](#spaces)), <a id="ref-for-segment-break①③"></a>segment breaks are instead transformed into a preserved line feed (U+000A).

Tests

- [white-space-008.xht](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/CSS2/text/white-space-008.xht) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/CSS2/text/white-space-008.xht) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/CSS2/text/white-space-008.xht)
- [white-space-processing-016.xht](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/CSS2/text/white-space-processing-016.xht) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/CSS2/text/white-space-processing-016.xht) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/CSS2/text/white-space-processing-016.xht)
- [white-space-processing-017.xht](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/CSS2/text/white-space-processing-017.xht) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/CSS2/text/white-space-processing-017.xht) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/CSS2/text/white-space-processing-017.xht)
- [white-space-processing-018.xht](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/CSS2/text/white-space-processing-018.xht) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/CSS2/text/white-space-processing-018.xht) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/CSS2/text/white-space-processing-018.xht)

When <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space-collapse①④"></a>[white-space-collapse](#propdef-white-space-collapse) is <a id="ref-for-valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse⑦"></a>[collapse](#valdef-white-space-collapse-collapse), <a id="ref-for-segment-break①④"></a>[segment breaks](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) are <a id="ref-for-collapsible-white-space⑧"></a>[collapsible](#collapsible-white-space), and are collapsed as follows:

1.  First, any collapsible <a id="ref-for-segment-break①⑤"></a>[segment break](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) immediately following another collapsible <a id="ref-for-segment-break①⑥"></a>segment break is removed.
    Tests

    - [segment-break-transformation-unremovable-2.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-2.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-2.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-2.html)
    - [segment-break-transformation-unremovable-4.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-4.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-4.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-4.html)

2.  Then any remaining <a id="ref-for-segment-break①⑦"></a>[segment break](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) is either transformed into a space (U+0020) or removed depending on the context before and after the break. The rules for this operation are UA-defined in this level.

    Tests

    - [segment-break-transformation-unremovable-1.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-1.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-1.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-1.html)
    - [segment-break-transformation-unremovable-2.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-2.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-2.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-2.html)
    - [segment-break-transformation-unremovable-3.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-3.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-3.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-3.html)
    - [segment-break-transformation-unremovable-4.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-4.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-4.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/line-breaking/segment-break-transformation-unremovable-4.html)

    <a id="issue-c0106ca7"></a>

    <strong>Issue:</strong>

    [](#issue-c0106ca7) Should we define this for Level 4?

    Note: The white space processing rules have already removed any <a id="ref-for-tabs①②"></a>[tabs](#tabs) and <a id="ref-for-spaces②⑨"></a>[spaces](#spaces) around the <a id="ref-for-segment-break①⑧"></a>[segment break](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) before this context is evaluated.

<a id="example-e2e8c9a5"></a>

<strong>Example:</strong>

[](#example-e2e8c9a5) The purpose of the segment break transformation rules (and white space collapsing in general) is to “unbreak” text that has been [broken into segments](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#white-space-processing) to make the document source code easier to work with. In languages that use word separators, such as English and Korean, “unbreaking” a line requires joining the two lines with a <a id="ref-for-spaces③⓪"></a>[space](#spaces).

<figure>
<pre class="text"><code>Here is an English paragraph
that is broken into multiple lines
in the source code so that it can
be more easily read and edited
in a text editor.</code></pre>
<p>Here is an English paragraph that is broken into multiple lines in the source code so that it can be more easily read and edited in a text editor.</p>
<figcaption>Eliminating a line break in English requires maintaining a <a id="ref-for-spaces③①"></a><a href="#spaces">space</a> in its place.</figcaption>
</figure>

In languages that have no word separators, such as Chinese, “unbreaking” a line requires joining the two lines with no intervening space.

<figure>
<pre class="text"><code>這個段落是那麼長，
在一行寫不行。最好
用三行寫。</code></pre>
<p>這個段落是那麼長，在一行寫不行。最好用三行寫。</p>
<figcaption>Eliminating a line break in Chinese requires eliminating any intervening <a id="ref-for-white-space①⑧"></a><a href="#white-space">white space</a>.</figcaption>
</figure>

The segment break transformation rules can use adjacent context to either transform the segment break into a space or eliminate it entirely.

Note: Historically, HTML and CSS have unconditionally converted <a id="ref-for-segment-break①⑨"></a>[segment breaks](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#segment-break) to spaces, which has prevented content authored in languages such as Chinese from being able to break lines within the source. Thus UA heuristics need to be conservative about where they discard <a id="ref-for-segment-break②⓪"></a>segment breaks even as they strive to improve support for such languages.

#### <a id="hyphenate-line-limits"></a>6.3.5.  Hyphenation Line Limits: the <a id="ref-for-propdef-hyphenate-limit-lines"></a>[hyphenate-limit-lines](#propdef-hyphenate-limit-lines) and <a id="ref-for-propdef-hyphenate-limit-last"></a>[hyphenate-limit-last](#propdef-hyphenate-limit-last) properties[](#hyphenate-line-limits)

Tests

This section lacks tests.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

| Field                                                                                    | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:</strong>                                                                   | <a id="propdef-hyphenate-limit-lines"></a><strong>hyphenate-limit-lines</strong>                                                                                                                             |
| <strong>[Value:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs)</strong>                  | no-limit <a id="ref-for-comb-one④①"></a>[\|](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-values-4/#comb-one) <a id="ref-for-integer-value②"></a>[\<integer \[0,∞\]\>](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-values-4/#integer-value) |
| <strong>[Initial:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#initial-values)</strong>           | no-limit                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| <strong>[Applies to:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#applies-to)</strong>            | block containers                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| <strong>[Inherited:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#inherited-property)</strong>     | yes                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| <strong>[Percentages:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#percentages)</strong>           | n/a                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| <strong>[Computed value:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#computed)</strong>          | specified keyword or integer                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| <strong>[Canonical order:](https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom/#serializing-css-values)</strong> | per grammar                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| <strong>[Animation type:](https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations/#animation-type)</strong> | by computed value type                                                                                                                                                                                       |

This property indicates the maximum number of successive hyphenated lines in an element. The <a id="ref-for-valdef-dynamic-range-limit-no-limit"></a>[no-limit](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-color-hdr-1/#valdef-dynamic-range-limit-no-limit) value means that there is no limit.

In some cases, user agents may not be able to honor the specified value. (See overflow-wrap.) It is not defined whether hyphenation introduced by such emergency breaking influences nearby hyphenation points.

| Field                                                                                    | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| <strong>Name:</strong>                                                                   | <a id="propdef-hyphenate-limit-last"></a><strong>hyphenate-limit-last</strong>                                                                                                                                            |
| <strong>[Value:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#value-defs)</strong>                  | none <a id="ref-for-comb-one④②"></a>[\|](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-values-4/#comb-one) always <a id="ref-for-comb-one④③"></a>\| column <a id="ref-for-comb-one④④"></a>\| page <a id="ref-for-comb-one④⑤"></a>\| spread |
| <strong>[Initial:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#initial-values)</strong>           | none                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| <strong>[Applies to:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#applies-to)</strong>            | <a id="ref-for-block-container⑤"></a>[block containers](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-display-4/#block-container)                                                                                                          |
| <strong>[Inherited:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#inherited-property)</strong>     | yes                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| <strong>[Percentages:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-values/#percentages)</strong>           | n/a                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| <strong>[Computed value:](https://www.w3.org/TR/css-cascade/#computed)</strong>          | specified keyword                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| <strong>[Canonical order:](https://www.w3.org/TR/cssom/#serializing-css-values)</strong> | per grammar                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| <strong>[Animation type:](https://www.w3.org/TR/web-animations/#animation-type)</strong> | discrete                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

This property indicates hyphenation behavior at the end of elements, column, pages, and spreads. A spread is a set of two pages that are visible to the reader at the same time. Values have the following meanings:

<strong><a id="valdef-hyphenate-limit-lines-none"></a><strong>none</strong></strong>

No restrictions imposed.

<strong><a id="valdef-hyphenate-limit-lines-always"></a><strong>always</strong></strong>

The last full line of the element, or the last line before any column, page, or spread break inside the element should not be hyphenated.

<strong><a id="valdef-hyphenate-limit-lines-column"></a><strong>column</strong></strong>

The last line before any column, page, or spread break inside the element should not be hyphenated.

<strong><a id="valdef-hyphenate-limit-lines-page"></a><strong>page</strong></strong>

The last line before page or spread break inside the element should not be hyphenated.

<strong><a id="valdef-hyphenate-limit-lines-spread"></a><strong>spread</strong></strong>

The last line before any spread break inside the element should not be hyphenated.

<a id="example-3e572423"></a>

<strong>Example:</strong>

[](#example-3e572423)

``` text
p { hyphenate-limit-last: always }
div.chapter {	hyphenate-limit-last: spread }
```

<a id="example-72bf355c"></a>

<strong>Example:</strong>

[](#example-72bf355c)

A paragraph may be formatted like this when <a id="ref-for-propdef-hyphenate-limit-last①"></a>[hyphenate-limit-last: none](#propdef-hyphenate-limit-last) is set:

``` text
This is just a
simple example
to show Antarc-
tica.
```

With 'hyphenate-limit-last: always' one would get:

``` text
This is just a
simple example
to        show
Antarctica.
```

- [overflow-wrap-anywhere-inline-002.tentative.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-anywhere-inline-002.tentative.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-anywhere-inline-002.tentative.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-anywhere-inline-002.tentative.html)

This property specifies whether the UA may break at otherwise disallowed points within a line to prevent overflow, when an otherwise-unbreakable string is too long to fit within the line box. It only has an effect when <a id="ref-for-propdef-white-space⑨"></a>[white-space](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-white-space) allows <a id="ref-for-wrapping⑤"></a>[wrapping](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#wrapping). Possible values:

Tests

- [overflow-wrap-002.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-002.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-002.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-002.html)

<strong><a id="valdef-overflow-wrap-normal"></a><strong>normal</strong></strong>

Lines may break only at allowed break points. However, the restrictions introduced by <a id="ref-for-propdef-word-break②⓪"></a>[word-break: keep-all](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-word-break) may be relaxed to match <a id="ref-for-propdef-word-break②①"></a>word-break: normal if there are no otherwise-acceptable break points in the line.

Tests

- [overflow-wrap-004.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-004.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-004.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-004.html)
- [overflow-wrap-normal-keep-all-001.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-normal-keep-all-001.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-normal-keep-all-001.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/overflow-wrap/overflow-wrap-normal-keep-all-001.html)

Also, the restrictions introduced by <a id="ref-for-propdef-word-break②②"></a>[word-break: auto-phrase](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-word-break) are relaxed if there are no otherwise-acceptable break points in the line:

- If suppressing <a id="ref-for-soft-wrap-opportunity④⓪"></a>[soft wrap opportunities](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#soft-wrap-opportunity) within a particular phrase would cause that phrase to overflow even when placed on an otherwise empty line, the user agent must fall back to the same <a id="ref-for-soft-wrap-opportunity④①"></a>soft wrap opportunities as <a id="ref-for-valdef-word-break-normal⑦"></a>[normal](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#valdef-word-break-normal) within that phrase.
  Tests

  - [word-break-auto-phrase-009.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/word-break/auto-phrase/word-break-auto-phrase-009.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/word-break/auto-phrase/word-break-auto-phrase-009.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/word-break/auto-phrase/word-break-auto-phrase-009.html)
  - [word-break-auto-phrase-overflow-001.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/word-break/auto-phrase/word-break-auto-phrase-overflow-001.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/word-break/auto-phrase/word-break-auto-phrase-overflow-001.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/word-break/auto-phrase/word-break-auto-phrase-overflow-001.html)
- If that is not enough to prevent overflow, suppression of <a id="ref-for-hyphenation-opportunity①⓪"></a>[hyphenation opportunities](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#hyphenation-opportunity) must also be abandoned within each line that would overflow.
  Tests

  - [word-break-auto-phrase-008.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/word-break/auto-phrase/word-break-auto-phrase-008.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/word-break/auto-phrase/word-break-auto-phrase-008.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/word-break/auto-phrase/word-break-auto-phrase-008.html)
- As an intermediary measure, user agents may also detect multiple levels of phrases, choosing to shorter ones (possibly down to individual words) when longer ones would lead to overflow.

The <a id="ref-for-soft-wrap-opportunity④②"></a>[soft wrap opportunities](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#soft-wrap-opportunity) obtained by relaxing the restrictions introduced by <a id="ref-for-propdef-word-break②③"></a>[word-break: keep-all](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-word-break) and <a id="ref-for-propdef-word-break②④"></a>word-break: auto-phrase are <em>not</em> considered when calculating <a id="ref-for-min-content①"></a>[min-content intrinsic sizes](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-sizing-3/#min-content).

<strong><a id="valdef-overflow-wrap-anywhere"></a><strong>anywhere</strong></strong>

An otherwise unbreakable sequence of <a id="ref-for-character①①"></a>[characters](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#character) may be broken at an arbitrary point if there are no otherwise-acceptable break points in the line. Shaping characters are still shaped as if the word were not broken, and grapheme clusters must stay together as one unit. No hyphenation character is inserted at the break point. <a id="ref-for-soft-wrap-opportunity④③"></a>[Soft wrap opportunities](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#soft-wrap-opportunity) introduced by <a id="ref-for-valdef-overflow-wrap-anywhere"></a>[anywhere](#valdef-overflow-wrap-anywhere) <em>are considered</em> when calculating <a id="ref-for-min-content②"></a>[min-content intrinsic sizes](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-sizing-3/#min-content).

In the case of <a id="ref-for-propdef-word-break②⑤"></a>[word-break: auto-phrase](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-word-break), these additional <a id="ref-for-soft-wrap-opportunity④④"></a>[soft wrap opportunities](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#soft-wrap-opportunity) are only introduced if relaxing the restrictions introduced by <a id="ref-for-propdef-word-break②⑥"></a>word-break: auto-phrase as described in <a id="ref-for-propdef-overflow-wrap⑤"></a>[overflow-wrap: normal](#propdef-overflow-wrap) is insufficient to prevent overflow.

<strong><a id="valdef-overflow-wrap-break-word"></a><strong>break-word</strong></strong>

As for <a id="ref-for-valdef-overflow-wrap-anywhere①"></a>[anywhere](#valdef-overflow-wrap-anywhere) except that <a id="ref-for-soft-wrap-opportunity④⑤"></a>[soft wrap opportunities](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#soft-wrap-opportunity) introduced by <a id="ref-for-valdef-overflow-wrap-break-word"></a>[break-word](#valdef-overflow-wrap-break-word) are <em>not</em> considered when calculating <a id="ref-for-min-content③"></a>[min-content intrinsic sizes](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-sizing-3/#min-content).

<a id="issue-86c3418a"></a>

<strong>Issue:</strong>

[](#issue-86c3418a) Do we need to add a <code>none</code> value to <a id="ref-for-propdef-overflow-wrap⑥"></a>[overflow-wrap](#propdef-overflow-wrap) to opt out of relaxing the <a id="ref-for-valdef-word-break-keep-all②"></a>[keep-all](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#valdef-word-break-keep-all) and <a id="ref-for-valdef-word-break-auto-phrase⑤"></a>[auto-phrase](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#valdef-word-break-auto-phrase) restrictions as allowed by <a id="ref-for-propdef-overflow-wrap⑦"></a>overflow-wrap: normal?

For legacy reasons, UAs must treat <a id="propdef-word-wrap"></a><strong>word-wrap</strong> as a <a id="ref-for-legacy-name-alias"></a>[legacy name alias](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-cascade-5/#legacy-name-alias) of the <a id="ref-for-propdef-overflow-wrap⑧"></a>[overflow-wrap](#propdef-overflow-wrap) property.

### <a id="text-align-property"></a>7.1.  Text Alignment: the <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-align"></a>[text-align](#propdef-text-align) shorthand[](#text-align-property)

Tests

This section has partial test coverage. Missing tests: \* tests for the \< \> value \* If (after justification, if any) the inline contents of a line box are too long to fit within it, then the contents are start-aligned: any content that doesn’t fit overflows the line box’s end edge. \* “Values other than justify-all or match-parent are assigned to text-align-all and reset text-align-last to auto.”, and being a shorthand in general

This <a id="ref-for-shorthand-property①"></a>[shorthand property](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-cascade-5/#shorthand-property) sets the <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-align-all"></a>[text-align-all](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-text-align-all) and <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-align-last"></a>[text-align-last](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-text-align-last) properties and describes how the inline-level content of a block is aligned along the inline axis if the content does not completely fill the line box. Values other than <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-justify-all"></a>[justify-all](#valdef-text-align-justify-all) or <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-match-parent"></a>[match-parent](#valdef-text-align-match-parent) are assigned to <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-align-all①"></a>text-align-all and reset <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-align-last①"></a>text-align-last to <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-last-auto"></a>[auto](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#valdef-text-align-last-auto).

Values have the following meanings:

<strong><a id="valdef-text-align-start"></a><strong>start</strong></strong>

Inline-level content is aligned to the <a id="ref-for-css-start"></a>[start](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#css-start) edge of the line box.

<strong><a id="valdef-text-align-left"></a><strong>left</strong></strong>

Inline-level content is aligned to the <a id="ref-for-line-left"></a>[line-left](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#line-left) edge of the line box. (In vertical writing modes, this can be either the physical top or bottom, depending on <a id="ref-for-propdef-writing-mode"></a>[writing-mode](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#propdef-writing-mode).) [\[CSS-WRITING-MODES-4\]](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#biblio-css-writing-modes-4)

<strong><a id="valdef-text-align-right"></a><strong>right</strong></strong>

Inline-level content is aligned to the <a id="ref-for-line-right"></a>[line-right](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#line-right) edge of the line box. (In vertical writing modes, this can be either the physical top or bottom, depending on <a id="ref-for-propdef-writing-mode①"></a>[writing-mode](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#propdef-writing-mode).) [\[CSS-WRITING-MODES-4\]](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#biblio-css-writing-modes-4)

<strong><a id="valdef-text-align-center"></a><strong>center</strong></strong>

Inline-level content is centered within the line box.

<strong><a id="valdef-text-align-string"></a><strong><a id="ref-for-string-value③"></a>[\<string\>](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-values-4/#string-value)</strong></strong>

The string must be a single character; otherwise the declaration is invalid and must be [ignored](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS21/conform.html#ignore). When applied to a table cell, specifies the <a id="valdef-text-align-alignment-character"></a><strong>alignment character</strong> around which the cell’s contents will align. See [below](#character-alignment) for further details and how this value combines with keywords.

<strong><a id="valdef-text-align-justify"></a><strong>justify</strong></strong>

Text is justified according to the method specified by the <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-justify"></a>[text-justify](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-text-justify) property, in order to exactly fill the line box. Unless otherwise specified by <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-align-last②"></a>[text-align-last](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-text-align-last), the last line before a <a id="ref-for-forced-line-break①②"></a>[forced line break](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#forced-line-break) is <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-start"></a>[start](#valdef-text-align-start)-aligned.

<strong><a id="valdef-text-align-match-parent"></a><strong>match-parent</strong></strong>

This value behaves the same as <a id="ref-for-valdef-all-inherit"></a>[inherit](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-cascade-5/#valdef-all-inherit) (computes to its parent’s computed value) except that an <a id="ref-for-inherited-value"></a>[inherited value](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-cascade-5/#inherited-value) of <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-start①"></a>[start](#valdef-text-align-start) or <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-end"></a>[end](#valdef-text-align-end) is interpreted against the parent’s <a id="ref-for-propdef-direction"></a>[direction](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-3/#propdef-direction) value and results in a computed value of either <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-left"></a>[left](#valdef-text-align-left) or <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-right"></a>[right](#valdef-text-align-right). Computes to <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-start②"></a>start when specified on the <a id="ref-for-root-element"></a>[root element](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-display-4/#root-element).

Tests

- [text-align-match-parent-05.html](https://wpt.fyi/results/css/css-text/text-align/text-align-match-parent-05.html) [(live test)](http://wpt.live/css/css-text/text-align/text-align-match-parent-05.html) [(source)](https://github.com/web-platform-tests/wpt/blob/master/css/css-text/text-align/text-align-match-parent-05.html)

A block of text is a stack of <a id="ref-for-line-box"></a>[line boxes](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-inline-3/#line-box). This property specifies how the inline-level boxes within each line box align with respect to the start and end sides of the line box. Alignment is not with respect to the [viewport](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSS2/visuren.html#viewport) or containing block.

In the case of <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-justify①"></a>[justify](#valdef-text-align-justify), the UA may stretch or shrink any inline boxes by [adjusting](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#text-justify-property) their text. (See <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-justify①"></a>[text-justify](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#propdef-text-justify).) If an element’s <a id="ref-for-white-space②⓪"></a>[white space](#white-space) is not <a id="ref-for-collapsible-white-space⑨"></a>[collapsible](#collapsible-white-space), then the UA is not required to adjust its text for the purpose of justification and may instead treat the text as having no <a id="ref-for-justification-opportunity①"></a>[justification opportunities](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#justification-opportunity). If the UA chooses to adjust the text, then it must ensure that <a id="ref-for-tab-stop②"></a>[tab stops](#tab-stop) continue to line up as required by the [white space processing rules](#white-space-rules).

If (after justification, if any) the inline contents of a line box are too long to fit within it, then the contents are <a id="ref-for-css-start①"></a>[start](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#css-start)-aligned: any content that doesn’t fit overflows the line box’s <a id="ref-for-css-end①"></a>[end](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#css-end) edge.

See [§ 9.3 Bidirectionality and Line Boxes](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-text-4/#bidi-linebox) for details on how to determine the <a id="ref-for-css-start②"></a>[start](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#css-start) and <a id="ref-for-css-end②"></a>[end](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-writing-modes-4/#css-end) edges of a line box.

### <a id="character-alignment"></a>7.2.  Character-based Alignment in a Table Column[](#character-alignment)

Tests

This section lacks tests.

------------------------------------------------------------------------

When multiple cells in a column have an alignment character specified, the alignment character of each such cell in the column is centered along a single column-parallel axis and the rest of the text in the column shifted accordingly. (Note that the strings do not have to be the same for each cell, although they usually are.)

<a id="issue-97356692"></a>

<strong>Issue:</strong>

[](#issue-97356692)Is this intended to say that it’s the centers of the alignment characters that should be aligned? It’s not clear that’s what it says, but that (or a different behavior) needs to be specified, to describe what happens when different occurrences of the alignment character are in different fonts. (Further, is that the intended behavior? Probably the most significant use case to consider is bold vs. non-bold text, which only varies slightly in width.) \[[feedback](https://lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/www-style/2016Jan/0233.html)\] \[minutes face-to-face 2016-02-02 10:00 AM\]

<a id="example-6a3cc5d3"></a>

<strong>Example:</strong>

[](#example-6a3cc5d3)

The following style sheet:

``` text
TD { text-align: "." center }
```

will cause the column of dollar figures in the following HTML table:

``` text
<TABLE>
<COL width="40">
<TR> <TH>Long distance calls
<TR> <TD> $1.30
<TR> <TD> $2.50
<TR> <TD> $10.80
<TR> <TD> $111.01
<TR> <TD> $85.
<TR> <TD> N/A
<TR> <TD> $.05
<TR> <TD> $.06
</TABLE>
```

to align along the decimal point. The table might be rendered as follows:

``` text
+---------------------+
| Long distance calls |
+---------------------+
|         $1.30       |
|         $2.50       |
|        $10.80       |
|       $111.01       |
|        $85.         |
|        N/A          |
|          $.05       |
|          $.06       |
+---------------------+
```

A keyword value may be specified in conjunction with the <a id="ref-for-string-value④"></a>[\<string\>](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-values-4/#string-value) value; if it is not given, it defaults to <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-right①"></a>[right](#valdef-text-align-right). This value is used:

- when character-based alignment is applied to boxes that are not table cells.
- when the text wraps to multiple lines (at unforced break points).
- when a character-aligned cell spans more than one column. In this case the keyword alignment value is used to determine which column’s axis to align with: the leftmost column for <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-left①"></a>[left](#valdef-text-align-left), the rightmost column for <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-right②"></a>[right](#valdef-text-align-right) and <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-center"></a>[center](#valdef-text-align-center), the startmost column for <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-start③"></a>[start](#valdef-text-align-start), the endmost column for <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-end①"></a>[end](#valdef-text-align-end).
- when the column is wide enough that the character alignment alone does not determine the positions of its character-aligned contents. In this case the keyword alignment of the first cell in the column with a specified alignment character is used to slide the position of the character-aligned contents to match the keyword alignment insofar as possible without changing the width of the column. For <a id="ref-for-valdef-text-align-center①"></a>[center](#valdef-text-align-center), the UA may center the aligned contents using its extremes, center the alignment axis itself (insofar as possible), or optically center the aligned contents some other way (such as by taking a weighted average of the extent of the cells' contents to either side of the axis).

Note: Right alignment is used by default for character-based alignment because numbering systems are almost all left-to-right even in right-to-left writing systems, and the primary use case of character-based alignment is for numerical alignment.

If the alignment character appears more than once in the text, the first instance is used for alignment. If the alignment character does not appear in a cell at all, the string is aligned as if the alignment character had been inserted at the end of its contents.

<a id="issue-f823e64b"></a>

<strong>Issue:</strong>

[](#issue-f823e64b)This needs to specify what text is searched for the alignment character. Is it only in-flow text whose containing block is the cell? Or is text within any in-flow descendants in the block formatting context established by the cell considered? If so, is it considered only as long as its <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-align②"></a>[text-align](#propdef-text-align) property is consistent with the cell’s? (Consistent in the alignment character, or fully consistent?)

<a id="issue-a4eeca60"></a>

<strong>Issue:</strong>

[](#issue-a4eeca60)This behavior of aligning as though the alignment character had been inserted at the end of the contents of the cell, combined with center-of-character alignment, will produce gaps on the end-side of lines that are alone on a line with <a id="ref-for-string-value⑤"></a>[\<string\>](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-values-4/#string-value) text-alignment, when none of the lines of the column has the alignment character, or, more importantly, when some of the lines do have the alignment character, but the column is not laid out at its max-content width. This is probably undesirable.

<a id="issue-c1a95522"></a>

<strong>Issue:</strong>

[](#issue-c1a95522)When the alignment character is inserted at the end of the contents, which font is used? (In particular, if the alignment character might be within a descendant block, is it the font of the block or the font of the table cell? Or if the insertion is at a forced break within an inline, does it use the font of the inline or the font of the block or cell?)

Character-based alignment occurs before table cell width computation so that auto width computations can leave enough space for alignment. Whether column-spanning cells participate in the alignment prior to or after width computation is undefined. If width constraints on the cell contents prevent full alignment throughout the column, the resulting alignment is undefined.

<a id="issue-f3db70cb"></a>

<strong>Issue:</strong>

[](#issue-f3db70cb)This should have a formal definition of how character alignment affects the min-content and max-content intrinsic widths (of table columns and all content that can be inside table columns). Max-content intrinsic widths need to be split into three numbers (assuming that it’s the centers of the alignment character that are aligned): one for widths without alignment characters, one for widths on the inline-start side of the center of the alignment character, one for widths on the inline-end side of the center of the alignment character. This operates based on all segments of text between forced breaks for max-content widths. For min-content widths, segments of text between forced breaks that contain optional breaks within them should clearly contribute only to the without-alignment-character width. However, it’s less clear whether all min-content widths should work this way, or whether segments between forced breaks that do not have optional breaks (and perhaps only those that actually contain the alignment character) should contribute to start-side-of-alignment-character and end-side-of-alignment-character min-content widths instead; this choice is a tradeoff between the meaning of min-content sizing of a table meaning the narrowest reasonable size versus honoring alignment characters in more cases. Another option might be to use whether line-breaking of optional breaks is allowed as a control for which behavior to use.

<a id="issue-b38ae96a"></a>

<strong>Issue:</strong>

[](#issue-b38ae96a)Formally defining the intrinsic width contributions of column-spanning cells with <a id="ref-for-string-value⑥"></a>[\<string\>](https://drafts.csswg.org/css-values-4/#string-value) values of <a id="ref-for-propdef-text-align③"></a>[text-align](#propdef-text-align) is a complicated (although straightforward) extension of the decisions made for intrinsic width contributions of non-column-spanning cells; this should also be formally defined. Contributions end up being made to the split intrinsic widths of the startmost or endmost column (whichever is used for alignment), and to the without-alignment-character intrinsic widths of the other spanned columns.
